Job Title: Representation of 3D objects using openGL and CPP 

Job Description:
•	Generate accurate representations of three-dimensional objects using application programming interface libraries and computer graphics development best practices
•	Create interactive graphics applications that respond to input devices
•	Develop a fully formed three-dimensional project that meets project requirements
Scenario
You work as a C++ and OpenGL 3D graphics developer for Triangle and Cube Studios. This company designs 3D worlds for clients and customizes them based on the varied needs presented by each particular client.
In this professional landscape, the demand for computational graphics and visualizations is continually growing. Your clients may come from the game industry looking for graphics and animations, the healthcare industry for medical visualizations, the entertainment industry for computer-generated imagery (CGI) and visual effects, business industries for 3D printing to create physical objects for applied real-world problem solving, and much more. When you are assigned one of these types of projects, you become responsible for writing code in OpenGL to create objects, apply texture, apply light, render, and control virtual environments relative to a virtual camera.
Your current project with Triangle and Cube Studios is to replicate a 3D version of a 2D image that you have been given by a client. Your client will later be 3D printing this to use as a preliminary concept for their business, so they only need you to create a simple approximation using a few basic shapes.
Directions
Using the image you selected in Milestone One, you will create 3D objects that represent the components and layout of that image. Although you have already begun to complete some of this work in your other milestones, during this stage of your final project, you will be refining and adding to your earlier submissions before bringing everything together. Note: You will be working on your 3D scene in Visual Studio but will also submit a written design decisions document discussing your approach throughout the process.
Specifically, you must address the following rubric criteria:
3D Objects
1.	Create low-polygon 3D representations of real-world objects. Make sure you have at least four completed objects in your 3D scene. At least one of the objects you create should be made using two or more primitive shapes. Note that the object you completed in an earlier milestone can count as one of your four. Use organized geometry and make sure that the polygons (triangles) on each 3D model are well spaced and connected. Minimize complexity and save 3D modeling time by setting the polygon count for your objects under 1,000 triangles. Remember to think in terms of simple shapes and ask yourself what basic 3D shapes go into making up each object in your scene. Four of the following basic shapes must appear at least once in your creation:
A.	Box
B.	Cone
C.	Cylinder
D.	Plane
E.	Prism
F.	Pyramid
G.	Sphere
H.	Tapered cylinder
I.	Torus
2.	Apply accurately projected textures to a 3D model. You must select at least two objects to texture. Note that you should have already textured one object in a previous milestone. If you use that object here, it will count as one of your two. The textures you select should be royalty-free images with resolutions of 1024 by 1024 pixels or higher. Please refer to the Sourcing Textures Tutorial linked in the Supporting Materials section for help finding images that you can use for textures.
3.	Apply lighting to create a polished visualization of 3D models. You must include a minimum of two light sources, and at least one of them should be colored. Note that the light you worked on in a previous milestone counts as one of your two lights. The light sources you create will need to capture all of the objects in the 3D world you are building. They should be positioned at locations that do not cause parts of the objects to appear dark when moving the camera around them. Include a point light for one of your two lights. You may use a directional light or spotlight. Make sure that you design lights in a way that helps create a final polished presentation. Use all components of the Phong shading model, including the following components:
A.	Ambient
B.	Diffuse
C.	Specular
4.	Place objects appropriately, using the X, Y, and Z coordinates, relative to one another in the 3D world. Match the photograph you selected as closely as possible by placing the objects in their proper locations. Note: The objects may overlap because all of the objects were likely initially placed at 0, 0, 0.
Navigation
5.	Apply horizontal, vertical, and depth camera navigation around the 3D scene. The camera will be traversing the X, Y, and Z axes. Make sure it captures all of the objects in your 3D scene. Use the code you created in an earlier milestone and then increase the radius of the camera’s orbit so it will project light on all of the objects. Add each object separately and then adjust the orbit radius or position of the camera each time. Use the following input devices:
A.	WASD keys should be used to control the forward, backward, left, and right motion.
B.	QE keys should be used to control the upward and downward movement.
6.	Apply nuanced camera controls to effectively view the 3D objects in the application. Changing the camera controls should allow the orientation of the camera to change even though its location has not moved. Focus first on pitch and yaw, but careful changes can be made to roll. Code for adjustments in the speed of the movement. This way, a user will have more control over how they explore the objects in the scene. Use the following input devices:
A.	Mouse cursor should be used to change the orientation of the camera so it can look up and down or right and left.
B.	Mouse scroll should be used to adjust the speed of the movement or the speed at which camera travels around the scene.
7.	Create perspective and orthographic displays of the 3D world. Use the tap of a keyboard key to allow a user to change the viewport display of all objects in the scene between orthographic (2D) and perspective (3D) views. Switch the function call to retrieve either the perspective or orthographic projection matrix. Keep the camera in the same orientation.
Best Practices
8.	Apply coding best practices in formatting, commenting, and functional logic. Complete the following items:

A.	Employ formatting best practices by providing program code that is easy to read and follows industry standard code formatting practices, such as indentation and spacing.
B.	Employ commenting best practices to ensure project source code is briefly and clearly explained using descriptive comments.
C.	Employ functional coding logic best practices to ensure the program runs as expected. You do not have to include Note that not everything should be written in a single function. Your work should be well modularized.
Reflection
9.	Justify development choices for your 3D scene. Think about why you chose your selected objects. Also consider how you were able to program for the required functionality.
10.	Explain how a user can navigate your 3D scene. Explain how you set up to control the virtual camera for your 3D scene using different input devices.
11.	Explain the custom functions in your program that you are using to make your code more modular and organized. Ask yourself, what does the function you developed do and how is it reusable?
Milestones
Milestone One: Project Proposal
In Module Two, you will submit your completed proposal that describes the 2D image you have selected to replicate and explains the basic shapes you will use to replicate the object in 3D. This milestone will be graded with the Milestone One Rubric.
Milestone Two: Beginning a 3D Scene
In Module Three, you will transform two of the basic shapes you selected in Milestone One. This milestone will be graded with the Milestone Two Rubric.
Milestone Three: Interactivity in a 3D Scene
In Module Four, you will incorporate input devices and camera movement into the 3D scene you created in Milestone Two. This milestone will be graded with the Milestone Three Rubric.
Milestone Four: Texturing Objects in a 3D Scene
In Module Five, you will apply texture to the 3D scene you previously created. This milestone will be graded with the Milestone Four Rubric.
Milestone Five: Lighting Complex Objects
In Module Six, you will apply lighting to the 3D scene you previously created. This milestone will be graded with the Milestone Five Rubric.
Final Project: Submission
In Module Seven, you will submit your completed 3D scene and a document explaining your design decisions. Your project should bring together and refine the elements of your other milestones. Your submission should reflect the incorporation of feedback gained throughout the course. This submission will be graded with the Final Project Rubric.
What to Submit
To complete this project, you must submit the following items:
3D Scene
Submit a completed ZIP folder with all of your code. Your ZIP folder will include one or multiple CPP files and the Visual Studio project files. Also, the ZIP folder must include an EXE file so that the assignment can still be verified if the submitted source code does not build successfully. Check for the EXE as a quick reference on your code’s functionality before you submit it. Review the Visual Studio Export Tutorial linked in the Supporting Materials section below for instructions on how to download the necessary ZIP folder.
Design Decisions

Deadline: Negotiable

I need someone to make DLD project.


The goal of this project is to design and implement a 3-way traffic light control system using
digital logic circuits. The traffic light system should control the flow of traffic at an intersection
with three directions: North-South, East-West, and potentially a dedicated turning lane. The
system will use a combination of timing circuits, counters, shift registers, and logic gates to
sequentially control the traffic lights, ensuring safe and efficient traffic flow.
The traffic light system should operate in a four-phase sequence:
1. 2. 3. 4. Phase 1: North-South Green, East-West Red
Phase 2: North-South Yellow, East-West Red
Phase 3: North-South Red, East-West Green
Phase 4: North-South Red, East-West Yellow
The system should ensure that:
• The green light is given to one direction at a time.
• The yellow light indicates the transition between the green and red lights.
• The red light indicates that traffic must stop.
The system should be designed with the following specifications:
• Traffic Lights: Each direction will have a Green, Yellow, and Red light. The lights can be
represented using LEDs or bulbs.
• Timing Control: Use a counter or timer circuit (such as a 555 timer) to determine how
long each light (Green, Yellow, Red) stays on for each phase.
• Sequential Control: Implement a sequential state machine using flip-flops, shift
registers, or a binary counter to ensure the correct progression through the four phases.
• Clock Generation: Use a clock pulse generator (such as a 555 timer in astable mode or a
binary counter) to control the timing intervals between each phase.


Deadline on 19th December.
Contact for price.

Input Flexibility: Supports grade input via CSV/Excel files, with customizable grading criteria based on user preferences.

* You must dedicate a total of **8 hours per day** to this role. Timing is flexible, but consistent effort is required to meet targets.  

**Understanding Credits & Submissions**:  
* **Credit**: A "credit" is earned when an expert you sign up completes the following process:  
  1. You source and sign up an expert.  
  2. The expert completes any required screening/introductory questions.  
  3. The expert is submitted to a client for consideration by the project coordinator.  
  4. The client books a call with the expert, and the call is completed (60 minutes).  
  - Each 60-minute call between your expert and the client counts as **1 credit**.  
  - If the expert is booked multiple times by the same or different clients, subsequent bookings count as **0.5 credits**.  

* **Submission**: A **submission** refers to each expert you source who signs up at our website, completes any required screening questions, and is submitted to the client for consideration by the project coordinator. A **submission** must meet these criteria to be valid.  

**Compensation Details:**  
* **Commission-Based:** This is a fully commission-based role with the following structure:  

**For every credit:**  
* **$80 per credit**  
* **$3 per expert submission** (An expert is considered submitted when they sign up on our website, complete any required screening, and are submitted to a client.)  

**Example Earnings:**  
* 10 credits = **10 × $80 = $800**  
* 30 expert submissions = **30 × $3 = $90**  
* **Total potential earnings for 10 credits and 30 submissions: $890**

manageable components.

choice (e.g., Flutter Navigation 2.0).

I'd like to create an app that counts the time each speaker, during a conversation, speaks.
Speaking time is important, spoken content not so much.
There should be also silence time.
The app should have a speaker recognizer (not speech recognition) tool.
Suppose there were only 2 speakers: A and B.
STA=Silence time between words of speaker A
STB=Silence time between words of speaker B
STM= Silence time  in the middle of speaker A and speaker B
TST= total Silence time = STA+STB+STM
WTA=time of spoken words by speaker A
WTB=time of spoken words by speaker B
CTA= conversation time of speaker A= WTA+STA
CTB= conversation time of speaker B= WTB+STB
TCT= total conversation time= CTA+STM+CTB.                      only android for the moment, open python or similar languages,  downloadable on app store for about 2 euros

Requirement Document for Automated Quotex Trading Bot via Telegram Signals

1. Project Title:

Automated Quotex Trading Bot with Telegram Signal Integration

2. Overview:

The goal is to develop a bot that automates trading on Quotex based on signals received via a Telegram bot. The system should:
	1.	Read and store up to 200-300 trading signals sent via Telegram.
	2.	Schedule trades based on a UTC +5:30 time zone.
	3.	Automatically log into Quotex and execute trades.
	4.	Test signals on a demo account first, with an option to switch to live trading.
	5.	Implement money management strategies like Take Profit (TP), Stop Loss (SL), and Martingale.
	6.	Run 24/7 without manual intervention.

3. Core Functionalities:

A. Telegram Bot for Signal Input
	•	The bot should be able to receive and store up to 300 trading signals in this format:

EUR/USD BUY 1min 12:30
GBP/USD SELL 1min 12:31
BTC/USD BUY 1min 12:32


	•	Each signal contains:
	•	Currency Pair (EUR/USD, GBP/USD, etc.)
	•	Trade Type (BUY/SELL)
	•	Trade Duration (1-minute expiry by default)
	•	Exact Trade Time (12:30 UTC+5:30)
	•	The bot should automatically store these signals and schedule them for execution.

B. Quotex Automation (Trade Execution)
	•	Auto-login to Quotex using stored credentials.
	•	Ability to select Demo or Live mode before execution.
	•	Place trades automatically based on signals at the scheduled time.
	•	Confirm successful execution and log trade results.

C. Switching Between Demo & Live Accounts
	•	The bot should have a toggle setting:
	•	DEMO_MODE = True → Trades on Quotex Demo Account.
	•	DEMO_MODE = False → Trades on Quotex Live Account.
	•	User should be able to change this setting easily before running the bot.

D. Money Management Strategies
	1.	Fixed Trading Amount (Default: $10 per trade, customizable).
	2.	Stop Loss & Take Profit
	•	Stop Loss: Stop trading if total losses exceed $100.
	•	Take Profit: Stop trading if total profits exceed $500.
	3.	Martingale Strategy (Up to 2 Steps)
	•	If a trade loses, the bot doubles the next trade amount.
	•	Example:
	•	Trade 1: $10 → Lost
	•	Trade 2: $20 → Lost
	•	Trade 3: $40 → Won → Reset back to $10
	•	The bot should only apply Martingale up to 2 times to minimize risk.
	4.	Alternative Money Management (If Needed)
	•	Percentage-based trades: Instead of a fixed amount, trade 1-5% of the balance per trade.
	•	Profit/Loss-based adjustment: Reduce trade size when losing, increase when winning.

E. Trade Scheduling & Execution
	•	The bot should store and execute signals at the exact scheduled time (UTC +5:30).
	•	If internet connection is lost, the bot should retry before skipping the trade.

F. Additional Requirements

✅ Must be able to run 24/7 on a VPS or cloud server.
✅ The bot should have an easy-to-edit configuration file (config.py).
✅ All trades and results should be logged for review.
✅ Provide an alert if Quotex login fails or a trade is missed.
✅ Ability to modify trade duration (default is 1 minute but should be changeable).

4. Development Plan & Next Steps

Phase 1: Setting Up the Telegram Bot
	•	✅ Create a bot using @BotFather.
	•	✅ Configure it to receive and store signals (up to 300).

Phase 2: Automating Quotex Trading
	•	✅ Implement login automation using Selenium.
	•	✅ Enable demo/live account switching.
	•	✅ Ensure correct trade execution at scheduled times.

Phase 3: Implementing Money Management
	•	✅ Add Stop Loss & Take Profit settings.
	•	✅ Add Martingale Strategy (Up to 2 steps max).
	•	✅ Allow fixed or percentage-based trading amounts.

Phase 4: Deployment & Testing
	•	✅ Test on Quotex Demo account first.
	•	✅ Verify all signals execute correctly at the scheduled time.
	•	✅ Deploy on a cloud server/VPS to run 24/7.

5. Deployment Instructions
	1.	Install Python and Required Libraries:

pip install selenium python-telegram-bot apscheduler


	2.	Run the Telegram Bot:

python telegram_bot.py


	3.	Run the Trading Bot:

python quotex_bot.py


	4.	Send Trading Signals in Telegram
	•	The bot will store them and execute trades automatically.
	5.	Switch Between Demo & Live Accounts
	•	Change DEMO_MODE = True or False in config.py.

6. Future Enhancements (Optional)

🔹 Custom Risk Management Strategies (e.g., trade based on previous performance).
🔹 Custom Alerts & Notifications (Receive messages on Telegram for each trade result).
🔹 Multi-Broker Support (Expand beyond Quotex).

7. Summary

✅ Handles 200-300 trading signals via Telegram.
✅ Trades automatically on Quotex at scheduled times.
✅ Starts in demo mode, with an option to switch to live.
✅ Uses Stop Loss, Take Profit & Martingale strategies.
✅ Designed to run 24/7 without manual intervention.

8. Next Steps
	•	Would you like step-by-step help setting this up?
	•	Do you need a freelancer to install and configure it?
	•	Would you like to host it on a cloud server for 24/7 execution?

1. *Data Ingestion*: Provide initial data to ChatGPT.

Expert required for 
tep 1: System Setup
Intersection Monitoring with RSU:

Deploy a Road Side Unit (RSU) at the intersection, equipped with sensors such as camera and LiDAR.
RSU monitors the environment, detects vulnerable road users (VRUs), and performs sensor fusion to enhance accuracy.
Vehicle Onboard Unit (OBU):

Vehicles have an OBU with sensors like camera and radar.
The OBU communicates with the RSU and other vehicles using PC5 (direct) communication when in proximity.
C-V2X Interfaces:

Use PC5 for direct communication (V2V, V2I, V2P) within a short range.
When vehicles move out of PC5 range, switch to Uu (cellular) communication for long-distance data sharing.
Pedestrian Communication:

Notifications are sent to pedestrians via the Uu interface using mobile devices.
Step 2: Multi-Sensor Fusion
Sensor Data Collection:

RSU collects data from camera (visual detection) and LiDAR (distance and depth detection).
Vehicles collect local data using onboard sensors.
Fusion Algorithm:

Implement a Kalman filter or a deep learning-based fusion model to combine sensor data.
The fused data improves object detection and classification, reducing false positives and false negatives.
Step 3: Reinforcement Learning Model
Multi-Agent Setup:

Define multiple agents for the system:
RSU Agent: Manages detection and message dissemination.
Vehicle Agent: Processes sensor data and safety messages to make driving decisions.
Pedestrian Agent (optional): Responds to notifications from the system.
Actor-Critic Model:

Actor: Generates optimal actions (e.g., send alerts, reduce speed, change lane).
Critic: Evaluates the actor's actions by assigning a reward based on safety and efficiency.
State, Action, and Reward Definition:

State: Sensor and communication data, including vehicle and pedestrian positions, speed, and message status.
Actions: Generate safety messages, adjust vehicle speed, notify pedestrians.
Rewards:
Positive rewards for preventing collisions and ensuring smooth traffic flow.
Negative rewards for communication delays, unsafe actions, or failed handovers.
Training:

Train the model using simulated scenarios in CARLA, SUMO, or similar environments.
Use frameworks like PyTorch or TensorFlow for reinforcement learning implementation.
Step 4: C-V2X Communication and Handover Mechanism
Safety Message Dissemination:

Use C-V2X protocols (CAM, DENM, MCM) to share real-time safety information.
The RSU broadcasts detected VRU data to nearby vehicles and infrastructure using PC5.
Handover Mechanism:

Implement a Mobile Broadband (MBB) handover to switch between PC5 and Uu communication without data loss.
Maintain synchronization by buffering messages during the handover process.
Step 5: System Performance Evaluation
Simulation Scenarios:

Simulate various intersection scenarios involving sudden pedestrian movements, vehicle lane changes, and traffic density variations.
Performance Metrics:

Measure system performance using key metrics, including collision rate, message latency, handover success rate, response time, and sensor fusion accuracy.
Result Analysis:

Analyze results to determine how well the system improves intersection safety.
Refine the reinforcement learning model based on simulation feedback.

Project Overview:
The Record Maintenance System is a web-based application designed to streamline the
management of student admissions data. The system offers functionalities for two user types:
students and admins. Students can sign up and submit admissions forms, while admins can
manage records. This project leverages Object-Oriented Programming (OOP) principles to
provide a robust and maintainable solution.
Key Features and Functionality:
● User authentication for students and admins.
● Students can register and fill out admissions forms with details such as name, CNIC, and
degree.
● Admins can view, delete, and update records, while the super admin has exclusive
authority to edit individual entries.
● Records are securely stored in a MySQL database for efficient retrieval and
management.
Object-Oriented Concepts:
● Classes and Objects: Represent entities such as users (students, admins) and records.
● Inheritance: Admin functionality extends from a base user class, with super admin
overriding specific permissions.
● Encapsulation: User data and permissions are encapsulated within appropriate
classes.
● Abstraction: Abstract classes or interfaces define shared behaviors among users.
Technologies and Tools:
● Programming Languages: Java (JSP, Servlets)
● Database: MySQL
● Frontend: HTML, CSS
● Development Tools: Apache Tomcat for deployment
Implementation Plan:

3. Backend Development: Implement servlets for data handling (Week 3).

5. Challenges and Potential Solutions:
● Data Integrity: Ensuring accurate data validation. Solution: Implement robust validation
mechanisms.
● Scalability: Handling large datasets. Solution: Optimize database queries and use
indexing.
Expected Outcome:
A fully functional web application where students can submit and manage their admissions data,
and admins can oversee these records efficiently using OOP concepts.

Extension to Domain Models and Use Cases you have to create Robustness Models. Robustness modeling bridges the gap between analysis and design, helping ensure that your system's functionality is correctly represented before moving into detailed design and implementation.

Instructions
For each of the three systems from your previous assignment:

Review Your Domain Model and Use Cases

Revisit your domain model and use case diagrams.
Ensure that all entities, relationships, actors, and system functionalities are accurately defined.
Make any necessary revisions based on feedback from your previous assignment.
Develop Robustness Models for Two Use Cases Per System
For two of the use cases identified in each system (choose the most complex or interesting ones):

Identify Boundary, Control, and Entity Objects:
Boundary Objects: Interfaces between the system and actors (e.g., UI screens, API endpoints).
Control Objects: Manage the flow of events in a use case (e.g., controllers, coordinators).
Entity Objects: Represent persistent data or business logic (e.g., database records, business rules).
Create Robustness Diagrams:
Draw robustness diagrams that illustrate how boundary objects interact with control and entity objects within the context of the selected use cases.
Show the flow of messages between objects to represent how data and control are transferred through the system.
Provide a Brief Explanation for Each Robustness Diagram

For each robustness diagram, include a short written explanation (3-5 sentences) that describes how the boundary, control, and entity objects interact and how the flow of control is managed in the use case.
System Descriptions
System 1: Online Bookstore
A popular online bookstore allows users to explore a vast collection of books across various genres. Visitors can search for specific titles, browse recommendations, and view detailed descriptions, including reviews from other readers. To make a purchase, a user must create an account, add items to their shopping cart, and complete a secure payment process.

Authors and publishers have access to a portal where they can upload new books, set prices, and manage stock availability. Once an order is placed, the system tracks its status, providing estimated delivery times and updates. Customers can also view their purchase history and manage their saved payment methods. Administrators oversee the platform, handling user issues, book listings, and general system maintenance.

System 2: Smart Home Automation System
A modern smart home automation system allows users to control and monitor household devices remotely. Through a mobile app or voice commands, homeowners can adjust lighting, regulate temperature, and lock or unlock doors. The system supports scheduling, enabling users to automate actions like turning off lights at bedtime or adjusting the thermostat based on outdoor conditions.

Security features include real-time monitoring through cameras and motion sensors, with instant alerts sent to users if unusual activity is detected. The system integrates with third-party smart devices, allowing seamless interaction with various brands. Access to the system is protected through authentication mechanisms, ensuring that only authorized individuals can modify settings or receive alerts.

System 3: University Course Registration System
A university relies on an online system to manage student course registration each semester. Students can browse available courses, check prerequisites, and enroll in classes. If a course reaches capacity, a waitlist feature notifies students when a spot becomes available. The system also supports course withdrawal and provides a view of the student’s academic schedule.

Professors use the system to upload course materials, post assignments, and enter grades. Communication tools enable instructors to send announcements and feedback to students. Administrators are responsible for managing course offerings, ensuring schedule accuracy, and maintaining student academic records. The system must provide a secure environment for handling sensitive information, such as grades and personal details.

Operating system:
Yes, the Raspberry is running Raspberry Pi OS 64-bit.
I cannot change the operating system, because other software packages requires that version.

Speed and limitation:
I prefer a solution which reads as fast as possible the actual state of gpio-input 17  when your 'ImshallRead17' function is called.
I understand that the OS/kernel is not a real-time OS and that other processes will have an affect.
This is a limitation we have to deal with and cannot change.
I prefer not to use generic high level drivers because they are not optimized for speed.

Objective:
The objective is to read the status of the gpio pin 17 as fast as possible.
(we can also use another alternative pin number if you prefer).
Edge triggered IRQ based reading does not fit either because the input signal might be very noise.
I'm interested to read the state when your function is called.
Higher level functions will call you your lower-level 'ImshallRead17' function a few times to validate the signal.

Challenging part:
I understand that there are gpio-read solutions based on 'Memory Mapped I/O' as described for other RaspberryPi-variants.
See for example: Memory Mapped IO:  https://www.codeembedded.com/blog/raspberry_pi_gpio/
Unfortunately you can not simply copy that code, because each Raspberry Pi model may have slight variations in its memory map, but the concept remains consistent.
I presume that for the RaspberryPi version 5 you have to modify it because it runs on the Broadcom BCM2712 SoC
See also: https://www.raspberrypi.com/documentation/

Please let me know if I can clarify any arising questions you might have.

Computing, and Electronics (SaaS)
Introduction
In the rapidly advancing fields of AI, robotics, quantum computing, and electronics, the need for 
an integrated platform that addresses the unique challenges faced by engineers and researchers is 
paramount. MetaCircuit AI is envisioned as a revolutionary Software-as-a-Service (SaaS) 
platform designed to simplify, innovate, and accelerate workflows across these domains. This 
proposal outlines the platform’s features, tools, and their specifications, along with examples to 
illustrate their application.
Platform Features and Tools
1. AI-Driven IC Design Tool
Purpose: Automate integrated circuit (IC) design, from conceptualization to production-ready 
layouts.
Features:
 Performance-Based Layouts: Automatically generate IC layouts based on specified 
constraints (e.g., power, area, timing).
 Built-in Validation: Design Rule Checks (DRC) and Layout vs. Schematic (LVS) 
validation tools to ensure production-readiness.
 Parameter Explorer: Allows parameter tweaks (e.g., voltage, frequency) with real-time 
visualization of their impact.
 Advanced Node Support: Enables design for cutting-edge process nodes (e.g., 3nm, 
5nm).
 Integration: Outputs in industry-standard file formats (GDSII, LEF/DEF) and 
interoperable with tools like Cadence Virtuoso.
Example: Designing a low-power, high-performance processor core for IoT devices with 
constraints on area and thermal performance 
2. Robotics System Designer
Purpose: Simplify robotic system creation with modular tools and integrated simulations.
Features:
 Modular Components: Libraries for motors, sensors, controllers, and communication 
modules.
 Programming Environment: Embedded coding support for Python, C++, and ROS.
 Simulated Testing: Real-world scenario testing using simulators like Gazebo or Unity.
 AI Optimization: Optimize control algorithms, such as PID and kinematic planning, 
with AI assistance.
 Integration: Compatibility with ROS, OpenCV, and microcontroller platforms like 
Arduino and STM32.
Example: Developing an autonomous mobile robot equipped with LiDAR navigation and 
dynamic obstacle avoidance.
3. Quantum Algorithm Optimizer
Purpose: Enable efficient quantum algorithm development with optimization and hardware 
integration.
Features:
 Visual Circuit Builder: Drag-and-drop quantum gate operations.
 Quantum Error Correction: Tools for fault-tolerant computing.
 Performance Metrics: Real-time metrics such as fidelity and coherence.
 Hardware Integration: Supports IBM Q, Google Quantum AI, and Rigetti platforms.
 Output Formats: Export quantum circuits in QASM or OpenQASM formats.
Example: Designing a quantum key distribution algorithm for secure communication using 
quantum entanglement.
4. AI Model Builder
Purpose: Provide tools for developing AI models tailored to engineering challenges.
Features:
 AutoML: Automatic architecture selection (e.g., CNN, RNN, Transformer) for the given 
task.
 Pre-Trained Models: Domain-specific libraries for electronic design, robotics, and 
quantum computation.
 Visualization: Real-time tracking of model training metrics.
 Output: Models exportable in ONNX, TensorFlow, or PyTorch formats.
Example: Building a deep learning model to predict thermal performance in multilayer PCBs.
5. Multi-Domain Simulation Tool
Purpose: Simulate interactions between systems across multiple domains (AI, robotics, 
quantum, electronics).
Features:
 Dynamic Simulations: Thermal, electrical, and mechanical simulations.
 Cross-Domain Coupling: Simulate interdependencies between robotics, AI, and 
electronics.
 Fault Injection: Test system robustness by introducing controlled errors.
 Scenario Playback: Save and replay specific simulation conditions for analysis.
Example: Simulating a robotic arm powered by AI vision and controlled by quantum-enhanced 
algorithms.

optimization.
Features:

10. Integrated Research and Knowledge Library
